### 25. n8n

_Source: `n8n_AI.json`_

#### Basic Information

**Name:** n8n

**Type:** automation

**Creator:** n8n GmbH, founded by Jan Oberhauser in 2019, headquartered in Berlin, Germany. Oberhauser is a solo founder who previously worked in visual effects (films like Maleficent) before pivoting to workflow automation.

**Description:** n8n is a fair-code workflow automation platform with native AI capabilities that lets users visually build, customize, and deploy complex automations connecting 400+ services. Unlike proprietary competitors (Zapier, Make), n8n can be fully self-hosted with Docker or Kubernetes at zero licensing cost, giving teams complete control over their data and infrastructure. Its built-in LangChain integration provides ~70 AI-dedicated nodes for building multi-step agents, RAG pipelines, and LLM-powered workflows. For entrepreneurs, n8n is the most cost-effective way to automate business processes at scale: a single workflow execution counts as one operation regardless of step count (vs. per-step billing at Zapier), and the self-hosted Community Edition is free forever with unlimited executions.

**Release Date:** October 2019 (initial open-source release). Latest major milestone: Series C funding October 2025.

**Url:** https://n8n.io

#### Technical Capabilities

**Underlying Models:** Multi-provider via native AI nodes: OpenAI (GPT-4o, GPT-4.1, GPT-4, GPT-3.5), Anthropic (Claude 3.5 Sonnet, Claude 3 Opus), Google (Gemini), Azure OpenAI, Hugging Face Inference API, Ollama (local models), and any model accessible via REST API through the HTTP Request node. Users can also run LLMs locally for complete data sovereignty.

**Customization Depth:** workflow-builder | code-extension. n8n combines a visual node-based workflow builder with deep customization: users can write custom JavaScript/Python in Function nodes, create custom nodes as npm packages, extend via community nodes (5,800+), and use the HTTP Request node to call any API. The LangChain integration allows composing AI agents with tool use, memory, and RAG components visually.

**Input Types:**

- text
- image
- audio
- documents
- CSV
- URLs
- JSON
- webhooks
- email
- database queries

**Output Types:**

- text
- API
- chatbot
- webhook
- email
- database writes
- file exports
- notifications
- spreadsheet updates

**Api Access:** Yes. Full REST API for workflow management, execution triggering, and credential handling. Webhook nodes for inbound triggers. Can expose any workflow as an API endpoint via the Webhook node.

**Deployment Options:** Self-hosted (Docker, Kubernetes, npm), cloud-hosted (n8n Cloud on Azure Frankfurt), air-gapped deployment on private networks, Docker Compose for quick setup, Kubernetes with Helm charts for enterprise scale. Queue mode supports multiple instances with workers for high availability. Handles up to 220 workflow executions per second on a single instance.

**Ai Model Flexibility:** multi-provider | bring-your-own-model. Users can freely choose between OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, Azure, Hugging Face, or self-hosted models (via Ollama or custom API endpoints). Different nodes in the same workflow can use different models. No vendor lock-in on AI model selection.

#### User Experience

**Target User:** developer | business_user | enterprise_team. Primary audience is technical teams and developers who want code-level flexibility with visual speed. Also accessible to power users comfortable with expressions and variables. Less suitable for non-technical business users compared to Zapier or Make.

**Learning Curve:** hours. Basic automations are achievable in 1-2 hours. The visual builder is intuitive but expressions and variables appear quickly, requiring some technical comfort. Mastering AI nodes, RAG pipelines, and self-hosting takes days to weeks.

**Setup Time:** 10-20 minutes. Cloud signup gives instant access. Self-hosted Docker setup takes ~10 minutes. First working workflow (e.g., email-to-Slack notification) achievable in 15-30 minutes using templates.

**Collaboration Features:** Git-based version control for workflows, role-based access control (RBAC), shared projects and workspaces, execution history and logging. Enterprise plan adds SSO (SAML/LDAP), audit logs, and advanced permission management. All plans include unlimited users.

**Template Library:** 900+ ready-to-use workflow templates available at n8n.io/workflows, covering categories like AI agents, CRM automation, social media, data processing, DevOps, and more. Templates can be imported with one click and customized. Community actively contributes new templates.

**Visual Builder Type:** drag-drop | code-editor. Node-based visual workflow builder where users connect trigger, action, and logic nodes on a canvas. Supports inline JavaScript/Python code blocks via Function nodes, expression editor for data transformation, and a built-in execution debugger for step-by-step testing.

#### Ai And Rag Capabilities

**Rag Support:** Built-in RAG pipeline support through dedicated nodes. Document ingestion from Google Drive, Notion, Confluence, local files, and URLs. Text chunking by tokens, characters, or headings. Embedding creation with OpenAI, Hugging Face, or custom models. Vector storage in Pinecone, Weaviate, Qdrant, Supabase, or Postgres with pgvector. Retrieval-and-generation workflows with configurable top-k context. As of v1.74.0 (January 2025), vector stores can be used directly as tools by AI agents.

**Agent Capabilities:** multi-step | multi-agent. n8n's AI Agent node (built on LangChain) supports: ReAct agents with tool use, OpenAI Functions agents, Plan-and-Execute agents, and custom agent types. Agents can use tools (calculators, web search, code execution, API calls, vector store queries). Agentflow V2 supports multi-agent orchestration with conversation memory, sub-agent delegation, and autonomous decision-making within defined workflow boundaries.

**Model Selection:** Multi-vendor with BYOM. Users freely switch between OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, Azure OpenAI, Hugging Face, and locally hosted models (Ollama, vLLM). Each AI node in a workflow can use a different model. No subscription required for any specific provider; users bring their own API keys.

**Prompt Management:** Prompt templates configurable per AI node with system prompts, user prompts, temperature, and max token controls. Prompts can reference dynamic data from previous workflow steps using expressions. No built-in prompt versioning beyond Git-based workflow version control.

#### Integration And Ecosystem

**Key Integrations:** Slack, Gmail, Google Sheets, Google Drive, Notion, Airtable, HubSpot, Salesforce, PostgreSQL, MySQL, MongoDB, Supabase, Webhook (generic), HTTP Request (any API), Telegram, WhatsApp, Discord, Microsoft Teams, GitHub, GitLab, Jira, Trello, Shopify, Stripe, Twilio, AWS S3, OpenAI, Anthropic, Google Gemini, Pinecone, Weaviate, Qdrant.

**Integration Count:** 400+ official/core nodes, 5,800+ community nodes (as of January 2026), and unlimited custom API integrations via HTTP Request node. Total ecosystem exceeds 1,000+ unique service connections.

**Mcp Support:** Yes. n8n has a native MCP Client Tool node that allows AI agents to connect to external MCP servers and use their exposed tools. Supports HTTP Streamable (recommended), SSE, and stdio transport modes. Community-maintained MCP server implementations also expose n8n workflows and node documentation to external AI assistants (Claude, Cursor, etc.).

**Webhook Support:** Yes. Full webhook support both inbound (Webhook node as workflow trigger) and outbound (HTTP Request node for callbacks). Any workflow can be exposed as a webhook endpoint. Supports authentication methods including basic auth, header auth, and JWT.

**Export Formats:** Workflows exportable as JSON files for backup, sharing, and version control. API endpoints via Webhook nodes. Execution data exportable. No standalone app export; n8n is a workflow engine, not an app builder. Docker images for portable deployment.

#### Pricing And Accessibility

**Pricing Model:** open-source (self-hosted free) + freemium (cloud). Self-hosted Community Edition is completely free with unlimited executions, workflows, and users under the Sustainable Use License. Cloud plans are subscription-based with execution-based billing. Pricing is per workflow execution, not per step or per user.

**Free Tier Limits:** Self-hosted Community Edition: completely free, unlimited executions, unlimited workflows, unlimited users, all 400+ integrations, all AI nodes. Cloud: no permanent free tier, but offers a free trial to get started. The Community Edition lacks SSO, RBAC, and premium support but is fully functional for production use.

**Paid Plans:** Cloud Starter: €24/month (2,500 executions, 5 concurrent workflows, 1 shared project). Cloud Pro: €60/month (10,000 executions, 20 concurrent workflows, 3 shared projects, environment variables, webhook auth, priority support). Enterprise: custom pricing (unlimited executions, SSO via SAML/LDAP, audit logs, advanced RBAC, dedicated support, SLA). Annual billing offers ~17% discount. All plans include unlimited users.

**Open Source:** open-core. Source code is publicly available on GitHub under the Sustainable Use License (SUL), a 'fair-code' license co-created by n8n. Anyone can self-host and modify for internal use. Reselling or offering n8n as a managed service requires a commercial agreement with n8n GmbH. Not a traditional OSI-approved open-source license.

**Self Hosting Option:** Yes, fully supported and a core differentiator. Deploy via Docker (recommended, 10-minute setup), Docker Compose, Kubernetes with Helm charts, or npm. Queue mode with Redis for horizontal scaling. Air-gapped deployment possible. Infrastructure cost for a basic production environment: $20-40/month on cloud providers (4GB RAM, 2 vCPUs recommended).

**Data Residency And Sovereignty:** Cloud: hosted on Microsoft Azure in Frankfurt, Germany (EU data residency by default). Self-hosted: complete data sovereignty, data stays on your infrastructure in any geography. GDPR compliant with Data Processing Agreement including Standard Contractual Clauses. SOC 2 Type II certified. Self-hosting enables full compliance with EU AI Act, NIS2, and sector-specific regulations. n8n processes no workflow data when self-hosted; only anonymous telemetry is sent (opt-out available).

#### Adoption And Market

**Market Position:** Leader in the open-source/fair-code workflow automation category. Positioned as the developer-friendly, self-hostable alternative to Zapier and Make. Rapidly emerging as the go-to platform for AI agent orchestration due to native LangChain integration and model flexibility. Strongest in the technical/developer segment; growing fast in enterprise.

**Notable Customers:** 3,000+ enterprise users globally. Notable customers include: Vodafone (security threat intelligence automation, saving ~£2.2M in operational costs), StepStone (200+ critical workflows daily, 25x faster data source integration), Delivery Hero (IT operations automation, saving 200 hours/month), Musixmatch (internal dev pipelines, saving 47 engineering workdays in 4 months), Fullscript (AI integration across organization), Icatu (insurance, first-mover product launches), Bordr (built $100K online business powered by n8n).

**Competitive Landscape:** Main competitors: Zapier (8,000+ integrations, easiest UX, most expensive per-task pricing, no self-hosting, limited AI), Make/Integromat (2,500+ integrations, visual builder, mid-range pricing, no self-hosting), Activepieces (open-source alternative, simpler), Windmill (code-first open-source), Temporal (developer workflow orchestration). Key differentiators: fair-code self-hosting, native AI/LangChain nodes, execution-based pricing (not per-step), Git version control, code extensibility, EU-headquartered with EU data residency.

**Ecosystem Lock In Risk:** low. Workflows are exportable as JSON. Source code is publicly available. Self-hosting means no dependency on n8n's cloud infrastructure. Standard integrations use common APIs and protocols. Community nodes are npm packages. The Sustainable Use License restricts reselling but not internal use or migration. Data remains under user control, especially when self-hosted.

#### Teaching And Classroom

**Class Project Idea:**

Project 1: 'AI-Powered Lead Enrichment Pipeline' (60 min) - Students build a workflow that triggers when a Google Form is submitted (simulating a lead capture form), uses an AI node (OpenAI/Claude) to analyze and score the lead based on their responses, enriches the data by querying a company database API, writes the enriched lead to a Google Sheet with a priority score, and sends a Slack notification for high-priority leads. This teaches: trigger-based automation, AI integration in business processes, data transformation, and multi-step workflow design. Deliverable: working workflow + 2-slide ROI analysis comparing manual vs. automated lead processing.

Project 2: 'Customer Support Email Auto-Responder with RAG' (75 min) - Students create a workflow triggered by incoming emails (via IMAP or Gmail node), which uses a RAG pipeline to search a knowledge base of FAQ documents, generates a draft response using an LLM, and either auto-sends for high-confidence answers or routes to a human via Slack for review. This demonstrates: email automation, RAG in practice, confidence thresholds, human-in-the-loop design, and the build-vs-buy decision for customer support AI. Deliverable: working email responder + analysis of when to use n8n vs. a dedicated support tool like Zendesk AI.

**Tutorial Resources:**

- https://docs.n8n.io/try-it-out/quickstart/ - Official quickstart: build your first workflow in minutes
- https://docs.n8n.io/courses/level-one/ - Official Level 1 course: comprehensive beginner training covering all core concepts
- https://docs.n8n.io/learning-path/ - Official learning path: structured progression from beginner to advanced
- https://docs.n8n.io/advanced-ai/intro-tutorial/ - Official tutorial: build an AI workflow in n8n (RAG, agents, LLM nodes)
- https://docs.n8n.io/advanced-ai/rag-in-n8n/ - Official RAG documentation: building retrieval-augmented generation pipelines
- https://n8n.io/workflows/ - 900+ workflow templates: browse and import ready-made automations
- https://blog.n8n.io/tag/tutorial/ - Official blog tutorials: step-by-step guides for specific use cases
- https://community.n8n.io/c/getting-started-with-n8n/docs-and-tutorials/6 - Community tutorials and documentation forum

**Student Prerequisites:** basic prompting. Students should understand what LLMs are and how to write basic prompts. No coding required for basic workflows, but comfort with variables and expressions is helpful. For AI-focused projects, understanding of API keys and basic data concepts (JSON) is beneficial but can be taught in-session.

**Session Mapping:** Session 2 (Prompt Engineering & No-Code Tools) - primary fit for hands-on workflow building with AI nodes. Session 3 (Framing & Managing AI Projects) - relevant for Build vs Buy discussions (n8n self-hosted vs. Zapier cloud vs. custom code). Session 4 (AI Business Models) - illustrative for open-core business model analysis and unit economics of automation.

#### Entrepreneurial Relevance

**Startup Applicability:** n8n is ideal for technical founders and small startup teams (seed to Series B) who need powerful automation without recurring SaaS costs. Best use cases: (1) Internal operations automation (CRM sync, lead enrichment, reporting pipelines) at zero licensing cost via self-hosting; (2) AI-powered features (customer support bots, content generation, data analysis) using the native LangChain nodes with any LLM provider; (3) MVP backend workflows where n8n replaces custom backend code for data processing, webhook handling, and third-party integrations. Particularly strong for European startups needing GDPR compliance through self-hosting. Less suitable for teams with zero technical capacity; those teams should consider Zapier or Make instead.

**Time To Mvp:** Hours to days. A basic automation workflow (e.g., form submission to CRM to Slack notification) takes 30-60 minutes. An AI-powered workflow (e.g., RAG-based support agent, lead scoring pipeline) takes 2-4 hours. A production-ready self-hosted deployment with multiple workflows takes 1-3 days. The 900+ templates significantly accelerate development by providing starting points for common patterns.

**Build Vs Buy Guidance:** Use n8n when: you want to self-host for cost savings or data sovereignty, your team has some technical capability, you need AI/LLM integration in workflows, you run complex multi-step automations (n8n's per-execution pricing is dramatically cheaper than Zapier's per-step billing), or you need Git-based version control for automation. Use Zapier when: your team is non-technical, you need maximum integration breadth (8,000+ apps), or you want the fastest possible setup. Use Make when: you want a visual builder with more integrations than n8n but don't need self-hosting. Build custom when: your automation requires sub-second latency, handles millions of events daily, or needs deep integration with proprietary systems that n8n nodes don't cover. Cost comparison: a workflow with 10 steps running 10,000 times/month costs ~€60/month on n8n Cloud vs. ~$250+ on Zapier; self-hosted n8n costs only infrastructure (~$30/month) for unlimited executions.

**Key Lessons:**

- 1. Self-hosting is a superpower for startups: The Community Edition is production-ready and completely free. A $30/month VPS gives you unlimited workflow executions, which would cost hundreds on Zapier. This is one of the few enterprise-grade tools where self-hosting genuinely saves money from day one.
- 2. Execution-based pricing changes the economics of automation: Unlike Zapier (per-step) or Make (per-operation), n8n charges per workflow execution regardless of complexity. A 20-step workflow costs the same as a 2-step one. Design complex, valuable automations without worrying about cost scaling with workflow complexity.
- 3. AI nodes are a genuine differentiator: n8n's ~70 LangChain-based AI nodes let you build RAG pipelines, AI agents with tool use, and LLM-powered workflows visually. This is uniquely powerful: you get the flexibility of a coding framework with the speed of a no-code builder. Use any model provider and switch freely.
- 4. Start with templates, customize aggressively: The 900+ community templates cover most common automation patterns. Import a template, then customize it to your exact needs using expressions, Function nodes, and custom API calls. This hybrid approach (template + code) is faster than building from scratch and more tailored than pure no-code.
- 5. Plan your data sovereignty strategy early: For EU-based startups, n8n's self-hosting capability provides GDPR compliance by design. Your workflow data never leaves your infrastructure. This becomes a competitive advantage when selling to enterprise customers or operating in regulated industries (healthcare, finance, insurance).

#### Confidence

**Data Quality:** High. Information sourced from n8n official website and documentation, GitHub repository, TechCrunch funding coverage, Sacra revenue estimates, official blog posts, and multiple independent review platforms. Pricing cross-referenced across 5+ sources. Funding data confirmed via TechCrunch, n8n blog, and Sacra.

**Cross Reference:** Funding confirmed via TechCrunch ($60M Series B, $180M Series C), n8n blog, Sacra, and Medium analysis. Pricing confirmed across official n8n.io/pricing, Zapier comparison, Latenode analysis, and ConnectSafely guide. GitHub stars (100k+) confirmed via GitHub and n8n community announcements. Customer case studies from official n8n.io/case-studies page.

**Caveats:** ARR figure (~$40M as of July 2025) is a Sacra estimate, not officially confirmed by n8n. The Sustainable Use License is not OSI-approved open-source, which matters for some enterprise procurement processes. Self-hosting infrastructure costs vary significantly based on scale and provider. Community node quality varies; not all 5,800+ nodes are production-ready. The platform evolves rapidly; AI features and pricing may have changed since the data collection date. n8n's learning curve is steeper than Zapier/Make for non-technical users.
